乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎病毒感染与肝细胞癌的关联:病毒学、组织病理学和分子证据的系统回顾

Association of Hepatitis B and Hepatitis C Virus Infections With Hepatocellular Carcinoma: A Systematic Review of Virological, Histopathological, and Molecular Evidence
作者： Ritu Saxena, Smriti Varshney, Mohd Sanan Khan, Mohit Bansal, Ruksar Syed, Patel Keta Sanjaybhai, Kuldeep Singh, Rajdeep Paul
摘要中文翻译慢性乙型肝炎 病毒 (HBV) 和丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 感染是肝细胞癌 (HCC) 的主要但生物学上不同的原因。本系统评价综合了将 HBV 和 HCV 感染与 HCC 联系起来的病毒学、组织病理学、基因组和分子证据,并比较了它们的肝癌发生的主要途径。该评价是根据 2020 年系统评价和荟萃分析的首选报告项目进行的指南。PubMed/MEDLINE 的检索时间为从开始到 2026 年 6 月 30 日,并辅以向后引文检索。使用病毒学、组织病理学、基因组、表观遗传学或分子结果评估 HBV 或 HCV 相关 HCC 的人类初步研究符合条件。在确定的 310 条记录中,在重复删除后筛选了 278 条,评估了 60 份全文报告,并在定性综合中纳入了 25 项研究。由于临床和方法学异质性很大,前瞻性 HBV 研究表明存在很强的病毒学风险梯度。乙型肝炎表面抗原和乙型肝炎 e 抗原联合阳性与调整后的 HCC 相对风险 60.2(95% 置信区间:35.5-102.1)相关,而 HCC 发病率 从每 100,000 人年 108 例增加到 1,152 例HBV DNA 类别。分子研究一致证明肿瘤组织中 HBV DNA 整合富集,端粒酶逆转录酶和其他癌症相关位点反复参与,以及插入突变和结构基因组改变。在 HCV 感染中,累积 HCC 风险从 HCV RNA 阴性个体的 1.1% 分别增加到低病毒血症组和高病毒血症组的 6.4% 和 14.7%。HCV 相关 HCC 与慢性病的关系更为密切坏死性炎症、氧化损伤、纤维化、肝硬化、病毒蛋白介导的信号传导和持续的表观遗传改变,而不是病毒 基因组 整合。组织病理学证据通常表明 HCV 相关性 HCC 中有更大的背景坏死性炎症和肝硬化,而 HBV 相关性 HCC 可能发生在纤维化程度较低或非肝硬化的肝脏中。因此,HBV 和 HCV 通过慢性肝损伤趋向于肝癌发生,但其主导作用不同HBV 具有显着的直接致癌成分,涉及病毒 DNA 整合和基因组不稳定性,而 HCV 相关 HCC 主要由炎症、纤维化和肝硬化相关致癌作用驱动,并具有额外的分子和表观遗传效应 ts. 晚期肝脏或持续性分子损伤发生后,病毒抑制或根除可降低但不能完全消除 HCC 风险。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
慢性乙型肝炎 病毒 (HBV) 和丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 感染是肝细胞癌 (HCC) 的主要但生物学上不同的原因。本系统综述综合了将 HBV 和 HCV 感染与 HCC 联系起来的病毒学、组织病理学、基因组和分子证据,并比较了它们的肝癌发生的主要途径。
证据基础与方法
该审查是根据 2020 年系统审查和荟萃分析指南的首选报告项目进行的。PubMed/MEDLINE 的检索时间为从开始到 2026 年 6 月 30 日,并辅以反向引文检索。使用病毒学、组织病理学、基因组、表观遗传学或分子结果评估 HBV 或 HCV 相关 HCC 的人类初步研究是合格的。在确定的 310 条记录中,筛选了 278 条重复删除后的记录,评估了 60 份全文报告,并将 25 项研究纳入定性综合。由于大量的临床和方法学异质性,未进行荟萃分析。
共识与结论
前瞻性乙型肝炎研究表明存在很强的病毒学风险梯度。乙型肝炎表面抗原和乙型肝炎 e 抗原综合阳性与调整后的 HCC 相对风险为 60.2(95% 置信区间:35.5-102.1)相关,而随着 HBV DNA 类别的增加,HCC 发病率 从每 100,000 人年 108 例增加到 1,152 例。分子研究一致证明肿瘤组织中 HBV DNA 整合富集,端粒酶逆转录酶和其他癌症相关位点反复参与,以及插入突变和结构基因组改变。因此,HBV 和 HCV 通过慢性肝损伤共同导致肝癌,但其主要机制不同。
争议与缺口
除了慢性坏死性炎症、氧化损伤、纤维化、肝硬化、病毒蛋白介导的信号传导和持续的表观遗传改变之外,该综述没有报告 HCV 相关 HCC 的具体机制。组织病理学证据通常表明 HCV 相关 HCC 背景坏死性炎症和肝硬化较多,而 HBV 相关 HCC 可能发生在纤维化程度较低或非肝硬化的肝脏中。晚期肝脏或持续性分子损伤发生后,病毒抑制或根除可降低但不能完全消除 HCC 风险。
科研与实践启示
除了 HCC 风险和机制的发现之外,该审查没有报告具体的研究或实践影响。

Association of Hepatitis B and Hepatitis C Virus Infections With Hepatocellular Carcinoma: A Systematic Review of Virological, Histopathological, and Molecular Evidence

Authors: Ritu Saxena, Smriti Varshney, Mohd Sanan Khan, Mohit Bansal, Ruksar Syed, Patel Keta Sanjaybhai, Kuldeep Singh, Rajdeep Paul
Original abstract (English)
Chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) infections are major but biologically distinct causes of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC).This systematic review synthesized virological, histopathological, genomic, and molecular evidence linking HBV and HCV infections with HCC and compared their dominant pathways of hepatocarcinogenesis.The review was conducted in accordance with the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses 2020 guidelines.PubMed/MEDLINE was searched from inception through June 30, 2026, supplemented by backward citation searching.Human primary studies evaluating HBV-or HCV-associated HCC using virological, histopathological, genomic, epigenetic, or molecular outcomes were eligible.Of 310 records identified, 278 were screened after duplicate removal, 60 full-text reports were assessed, and 25 studies were included in the qualitative synthesis.Meta-analysis was not undertaken because of substantial clinical and methodological heterogeneity.Prospective HBV studies demonstrated strong virological risk gradients.Combined hepatitis B surface antigen and hepatitis B e antigen positivity was associated with an adjusted relative risk of 60.2 (95% confidence interval: 35.5-102.1)for HCC, while HCC incidence increased from 108 to 1,152 per 100,000 person-years across increasing HBV DNA categories.Molecular studies consistently demonstrated enrichment of HBV DNA integration in tumor tissue, with recurrent involvement of telomerase reverse transcriptase and other cancer-associated loci, together with insertional mutagenesis and structural genomic alterations.In HCV infection, cumulative HCC risk increased from 1.1% among HCV RNA-negative individuals to 6.4% and 14.7% among low-and high-viremia groups, respectively.HCV-associated HCC was more closely linked to chronic necroinflammation, oxidative injury, fibrosis, cirrhosis, viral proteinmediated signaling, and persistent epigenetic alterations rather than viral genome integration.Histopathological evidence generally demonstrated greater background necroinflammation and cirrhosis in HCV-associated HCC, whereas HBV-associated HCC could occur in less fibrotic or noncirrhotic liver.HBV and HCV therefore converge on hepatocarcinogenesis through chronic liver injury but differ in their dominant mechanisms.HBV has a prominent direct oncogenic component involving viral DNA integration and genomic instability, whereas HCV-associated HCC is predominantly driven by inflammation-, fibrosis-, and cirrhosis-associated carcinogenesis with additional molecular and epigenetic effects.Viral suppression or eradication reduces but does not completely eliminate HCC risk after advanced hepatic or persistent molecular injury has developed.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) infections are major but biologically distinct causes of hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC). This systematic review synthesized virological, histopathological, genomic, and molecular evidence linking HBV and HCV infections with HCC and compared their dominant pathways of hepatocarcinogenesis.
Evidence base and method
The review was conducted in accordance with the Preferred Reporting Items for Systematic Reviews and Meta-Analyses 2020 guidelines. PubMed/MEDLINE was searched from inception through June 30, 2026, supplemented by backward citation searching. Human primary studies evaluating HBV-or HCV-associated HCC using virological, histopathological, genomic, epigenetic, or molecular outcomes were eligible. Of 310 records identified, 278 were screened after duplicate removal, 60 full-text reports were assessed, and 25 studies were included in the qualitative synthesis. Meta-analysis was not undertaken because of substantial clinical and methodological heterogeneity.
Consensus and conclusions
Prospective HBV studies demonstrated strong virological risk gradients. Combined hepatitis B surface antigen and hepatitis B e antigen positivity was associated with an adjusted relative risk of 60.2 (95% confidence interval: 35.5-102.1) for HCC, while HCC incidence increased from 108 to 1,152 per 100,000 person-years across increasing HBV DNA categories. Molecular studies consistently demonstrated enrichment of HBV DNA integration in tumor tissue, with recurrent involvement of telomerase reverse transcriptase and other cancer-associated loci, together with insertional mutagenesis and structural genomic alterations. HBV and HCV therefore converge on hepatocarcinogenesis through chronic liver injury but differ in their dominant mechanisms.
Controversies and gaps
The review did not report on the specific mechanisms of HCV-associated HCC beyond chronic necroinflammation, oxidative injury, fibrosis, cirrhosis, viral protein-mediated signaling, and persistent epigenetic alterations. Histopathological evidence generally demonstrated greater background necroinflammation and cirrhosis in HCV-associated HCC, whereas HBV-associated HCC could occur in less fibrotic or noncirrhotic liver. Viral suppression or eradication reduces but does not completely eliminate HCC risk after advanced hepatic or persistent molecular injury has developed.
Research and practice implications
The review did not report on specific research or practice implications beyond the findings on HCC risk and mechanisms.

对难民 慢性乙型肝炎 初级保健管理的看法:对维多利亚州墨尔本医疗保健提供者进行定性访谈的结果

Perspectives on primary care management of chronic hepatitis B in refugees: findings from qualitative interviews with healthcare providers in Melbourne, Victoria
作者： Amireh Fakhouri, Sara L. Newton, Lester Mascarenhas, Meredith J Temple-Smith
摘要中文翻译背景:在澳大利亚,医疗保健提供者在满足难民的特殊健康需求方面面临着重大挑战。健康的社会决定因素,包括有限的健康素养和财务状况,使难民获得 治疗 的条件变得复杂,例如 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB)。了解专门针对难民的全科医疗所采用的策略可以为治疗该人群提供指导。本研究的目的是探讨初级保健目前如何管理针对难民的慢性乙型肝炎护理,并确定潜在改进的领域。方法:2024 年 8 月至 12 月期间,我们对 9 名全科医生、4 名护士、1 名药剂师和 1 名传染病专家进行了 15 次半结构化访谈。我们使用了方便样本和滚雪球的方法;大多数参与者在澳大利亚维多利亚州墨尔本西部一家以难民为中心的全科诊所工作。所有访谈均被录音并转录;对数据进行了描述性分析。结果:我们研究中的初级医疗保健提供者认识到治疗慢性乙型肝炎难民面临的挑战,包括健康素养低以及提供和寻求护理的成本。然而,与医院服务相比,初级保健的好处包括社区融合和位置、融洽关系的发展以及口译服务的可用性。建议补贴初级保健提供者的培训和其他费用。结论:初级保健可能比医院服务更适合治疗慢性乙型肝炎难民。这些发现支持资助和改善初级保健中的 CHB 管理,以满足难民的需求,这将为这一弱势群体带来更好的健康结果。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究重点关注通过初级保健管理难民 慢性乙型肝炎 (CHB) 的挑战和益处。核心问题是初级保健如何更好地满足慢性乙型肝炎难民的独特健康需求,特别是在健康素养、获得护理和融入社区方面。
证据基础与方法
该审查基于对维多利亚州墨尔本医疗保健提供者的定性访谈。2024 年 8 月至 12 月期间,对 9 名全科医生、4 名护士、1 名药剂师和 1 名传染病专家进行了 15 次半结构化访谈。使用便利样本和滚雪球法来选择参与者,他们主要在墨尔本西部一家以难民为重点的全科诊所工作。所有访谈均被记录和转录,并对数据进行描述性分析。
共识与结论
研究中的医疗保健提供者承认治疗慢性乙型肝炎难民面临着挑战,例如健康素养低和经济障碍。然而,他们强调了初级保健的优势,包括社区融合、便利的地理位置、融洽的发展和口译服务。该研究的结论是,初级保健可能比医院服务更适合管理难民的慢性乙型肝炎,并建议补贴培训和其他费用以改善护理。
争议与缺口
该研究没有报告重大争议或相互矛盾的发现。然而,它也发现了证据差距,例如缺乏关于特定干预措施有效性的详细信息或政策变化对初级保健机构慢性乙型肝炎管理的影响的详细信息。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,改善难民慢性乙型肝炎的初级保健管理需要对医疗保健提供者提供有针对性的资金和培训。此外,需要进一步研究来评估这些干预措施的有效性,并解决影响难民健康结果的更广泛的系统性挑战。

Perspectives on primary care management of chronic hepatitis B in refugees: findings from qualitative interviews with healthcare providers in Melbourne, Victoria

Authors: Amireh Fakhouri, Sara L. Newton, Lester Mascarenhas, Meredith J Temple-Smith
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: In Australia, healthcare providers face significant challenges in meeting the niche health needs of refugees. Social determinants of health, including limited health literacy and finances, complicate refugees' access to treatment for conditions, such as chronic hepatitis B (CHB). Understanding the strategies utilised by a specialised general practice for refugees can offer guidance for treating this population. The aim of this study was to explore how primary care is currently managing CHB care tailored to refugees and to identify areas for potential improvements. METHODS: Between August and December 2024, we conducted 15 semi-structured interviews with nine general practitioners, four nurses, one pharmacist and one infectious disease specialist. We used a convenience sample and snowballing; most participants worked at a refugee-focused general practice in the west of Melbourne, Victoria, Australia. All interviews were recorded and transcribed; data were analysed descriptively. RESULTS: Primary healthcare providers in our study recognised the challenges of treating refugees with CHB, including low health literacy, and the cost of providing and seeking care. However, when compared with hospital services, the benefits of primary care include community integration and location, rapport development, and the availability of interpreter services. Subsidising primary care provider training and other costs were recommended. CONCLUSIONS: Primary care may be better positioned than hospital services to treat refugees with CHB. These findings support funding and improving CHB management in primary care to meet the needs of refugees, which will lead to better health outcomes for this disadvantaged population.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on the challenges and benefits of managing chronic hepatitis B (CHB) in refugees through primary care. The central question is how primary care can better address the unique health needs of refugees with CHB, particularly in relation to health literacy, access to care, and integration into the community.
Evidence base and method
The review is based on qualitative interviews with healthcare providers in Melbourne, Victoria. Between August and December 2024, 15 semi-structured interviews were conducted with nine general practitioners, four nurses, one pharmacist, and one infectious disease specialist. A convenience sample and snowballing method were used to select participants, who primarily worked at a refugee-focused general practice in western Melbourne. All interviews were recorded and transcribed, and data were analysed descriptively.
Consensus and conclusions
Healthcare providers in the study acknowledged the challenges of treating refugees with CHB, such as low health literacy and financial barriers. However, they highlighted the advantages of primary care, including community integration, convenient location, rapport development, and interpreter services. The study concludes that primary care may be better suited than hospital services to manage CHB in refugees, and it recommends subsidising training and other costs to improve care.
Controversies and gaps
The study does not report significant controversies or conflicting findings. However, it identifies evidence gaps, such as the lack of detailed information on the effectiveness of specific interventions or the impact of policy changes on CHB management in primary care settings.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest that improving primary care management of CHB in refugees requires targeted funding and training for healthcare providers. Additionally, there is a need for further research to evaluate the effectiveness of these interventions and to address the broader systemic challenges affecting refugee health outcomes.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

肝细胞癌中 HBV 和 HCV 归因的全球重新评估:对 878,083 名患者的系统回顾和荟萃分析揭示了最近的复发

Global Reappraisal of HBV and HCV Attribution in Hepatocellular Carcinoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis of 878,083 patients reveals a recent resurgence
作者： Baojiang Wen, Hao Wu, Duo Zuo, Wenna Jiang, Changsen Bai, Qianhui Hao, Jiayi Wang, Yue Shi, Li Ren
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景和目的 随着 疫苗接种 和 抗病毒药物 治疗的进展,慢性乙型肝炎 病毒 (HBV) 和丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 感染对肝细胞癌 (HCC) 的影响发生了变化。我们的目的是量化他们的全球贡献并评估地理和时间变化。方法 我们对报告 HCC 患者中 HBV 或 HCV 感染的观察性研究进行了系统回顾和荟萃分析。检索了 PubMed、Embase、Web of Science 和 Cochrane 数据库,检索时间从最初到 2026 年 2 月 19 日。使用随机效应模型来估计汇总比例。全球估计值是根据 GLOBOCAN 2022 的 HCC 发病率 对特定国家估计值进行加权而生成的。使用具有受限三次样条的非线性元回归来评估时间趋势。结果 共纳入 344 项研究,包括 878,083 名 HCC 患者。50.4% 的患者检测出 HBV 感染,25.8% 的患者检测出 HCV 感染。乙型肝炎病毒是东亚、东南亚和撒哈拉以南非洲地区的主要病毒病原,而丙型肝炎病毒在北非、中东、中亚和高收入北美地区的贡献最高。时间分析显示,HBV 相关 HCC 在前几十年有所下降,随后在 2010 年左右有所增加。HCV 相关 HCC 表现出非线性模式,在前几十年有所增加,并在 2005 年左右达到峰值后下降。HBV 和 HCV 混合感染占全球 HCC 病例的 4.4%。结论 尽管肝炎预防和 治疗 方面取得了进展,但全球范围内 HCC 的病毒病因正在发生变化。持续识别慢性 HBV 或 HCV 感染者并进行 监测 对于减轻全球 HCC 负担仍然至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述旨在量化 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 和丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 感染对肝细胞癌 (HCC) 的全球影响,并评估地理和时间变化。重点是将乙型肝炎病毒作为主要目标,丙型肝炎病毒被视为相关实体。
证据基础与方法
对观察性研究进行了系统回顾和荟萃分析,检索了从成立到 2026 年 2 月 19 日的 PubMed、Embase、Web of Science 和 Cochrane 数据库。使用随机效应模型来估计合并比例,并使用受限三次样条的非线性元回归来评估时间趋势。全球估计值是根据 GLOBOCAN 2022 的 HCC 发病率 对特定国家的估计值进行加权而生成的。
共识与结论
全球 50.4% 的 HCC 患者发现 HBV 感染,25.8% 的 HCV 感染。乙型肝炎病毒是东亚、东南亚和撒哈拉以南非洲地区的主要病毒病原,而丙型肝炎病毒在北非、中东、中亚和高收入北美地区的贡献最高。HBV 和 HCV 混合感染占全球 HCC 病例的 4.4%。尽管肝炎预防和 治疗 取得了进展,但全球范围内 HCC 的病毒病因正在发生变化。
争议与缺口
该综述指出,HBV 相关 HCC 最近出现复苏,在前几十年有所下降,随后在 2010 年左右有所增加。HCV 相关 HCC 表现出一种非线性模式,在前几十年中有所增加,在 2005 年左右达到峰值后有所下降。证据强调了地理和时间变化,但没有提供这些变化的详细机制。
科研与实践启示
持续识别慢性 HBV 或 HCV 感染者并进行 监测 对于减轻全球 HCC 负担仍然至关重要。研究结果表明,需要在 HBV 和 HCV 对 HCC 贡献较高的地区采取有针对性的干预措施。

Global Reappraisal of HBV and HCV Attribution in Hepatocellular Carcinoma: a systematic review and meta-analysis of 878,083 patients reveals a recent resurgence

Authors: Baojiang Wen, Hao Wu, Duo Zuo, Wenna Jiang, Changsen Bai, Qianhui Hao, Jiayi Wang, Yue Shi, Li Ren
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background and Aims The contribution of chronic hepatitis B virus (HBV) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) infection to hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) has changed with advances in vaccination and antiviral therapy. We aimed to quantify their global contribution and evaluate geographic and temporal variations. Methods We performed a systematic review and meta-analysis of observational studies reporting HBV or HCV infection among HCC patients. PubMed, Embase, Web of Science, and Cochrane databases were searched from inception to February 19, 2026. Random-effects models were used to estimate pooled proportions. Global estimates were generated by weighting country-specific estimates according to HCC incidence from GLOBOCAN 2022. Temporal trends were assessed using nonlinear meta-regression with restricted cubic splines. Results A total of 344 studies including 878,083 HCC patients were included. HBV infection was identified in 50.4% of patients and HCV infection in 25.8%. HBV was the predominant viral etiology in East Asia, Southeast Asia, and sub-Saharan Africa, whereas HCV contribution was highest in North Africa and the Middle East, Central Asia, and high-income North America. Temporal analyses showed a decline in HBV-associated HCC during earlier decades followed by an increase after approximately 2010. HCV-associated HCC demonstrated a nonlinear pattern, increasing in earlier decades and declining after a peak around 2005. HBV&HCV co-infection accounted for 4.4% of HCC cases globally. Conclusions The viral etiology of HCC is changing worldwide despite advances in hepatitis prevention and treatment. Continued identification and surveillance of individuals with chronic HBV or HCV infection remain essential for reducing the global burden of HCC.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review aims to quantify the global contribution of hepatitis B virus (HBV) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) infection to hepatocellular carcinoma (HCC) and evaluate geographic and temporal variations. The focus is on HBV as the primary target, with HCV considered a related entity.
Evidence base and method
A systematic review and meta-analysis of observational studies were conducted, searching PubMed, Embase, Web of Science, and Cochrane databases from inception to February 19, 2026. Random-effects models were used to estimate pooled proportions, and temporal trends were assessed using nonlinear meta-regression with restricted cubic splines. Global estimates were generated by weighting country-specific estimates according to HCC incidence from GLOBOCAN 2022.
Consensus and conclusions
HBV infection was identified in 50.4% of HCC patients globally, with HCV infection in 25.8%. HBV was the predominant viral etiology in East Asia, Southeast Asia, and sub-Saharan Africa, whereas HCV contribution was highest in North Africa and the Middle East, Central Asia, and high-income North America. HBV&HCV co-infection accounted for 4.4% of HCC cases globally. The viral etiology of HCC is changing worldwide despite advances in hepatitis prevention and treatment.
Controversies and gaps
The review notes a recent resurgence in HBV-associated HCC, with a decline in earlier decades followed by an increase after approximately 2010. HCV-associated HCC demonstrated a nonlinear pattern, increasing in earlier decades and declining after a peak around 2005. The evidence highlights geographic and temporal variations but does not provide detailed mechanisms for these changes.
Research and practice implications
Continued identification and surveillance of individuals with chronic HBV or HCV infection remain essential for reducing the global burden of HCC. The findings suggest the need for targeted interventions in regions with high HBV and HCV contributions to HCC.

基线乙型肝炎表面抗原和肝硬化在确定干扰素 治疗 持续时间方面的生物学解释

Biological interpretation of baseline hepatitis B surface antigen and cirrhosis in determining interferon treatment duration
作者： Ling-Feng Zou, Shao-Ding Yu, Yan Luo, Cheng-Long Wang
摘要中文翻译最近将基线乙型肝炎表面抗原和肝硬化确定为延长干扰素治疗的独立预测因子,提供了有价值的临床框架,但这些关联背后的生物学机制需要进一步阐明。在本意见综述中,我们不仅将这些因素解释为统计协变量,而且将其解释为病毒-宿主 平衡的病理替代物。我们认为,高基线乙型肝炎表面抗原可能反映了肝内根深蒂固的病毒 储存宿主,并可能创造一个富含抗原的环境,损害有效的 抗病毒药物 免疫力。同时,肝硬化可以更好地理解为一系列结构重塑,其中血管变化和肝窦开窗可能限制免疫 监测 并影响局部治疗途径。因此,可能需要(至少部分)超过 48 周的 治疗 持续时间来克服这些深刻的免疫学和结构障碍。我们主张将未来的预测模型从二元临床定义转变为病毒动力学和组织学形态的精细整合,以完善 慢性乙型肝炎 中的精准医学。

范围与问题
最近发现基线乙型肝炎表面抗原和肝硬化是延长干扰素治疗的独立预测因子,为临床提供了有价值的框架,但这些关联背后的生物学机制仍需进一步阐明。
证据基础与方法
在本观点综述中,我们认为这些因素不仅仅是统计学上的协变量,而是病毒与宿主平衡的病理替代标志。
共识与结论
我们提出,较高的基线乙型肝炎表面抗原水平可能反映了乙型肝炎病毒在肝内高度固着的储存宿主,并可能形成抗原丰富的环境,从而损害有效的抗病毒药物免疫应答。
争议与缺口
同时,肝硬化可能更好地被理解为一种结构重塑的谱系,在其中血管变化和窦状隙的破坏可能限制免疫监测,并影响局部治疗的可及性。
科研与实践启示
因此,可能需要(至少部分)超过 48 周的 治疗 持续时间来克服这些深刻的免疫学和结构障碍。

Biological interpretation of baseline hepatitis B surface antigen and cirrhosis in determining interferon treatment duration

Authors: Ling-Feng Zou, Shao-Ding Yu, Yan Luo, Cheng-Long Wang
Original abstract (English)
The recent identification of baseline hepatitis B surface antigen and cirrhosis as independent predictors of extended interferon therapy provides a valuable clinical framework, yet the biological mechanisms underlying these associations require further elucidation. In this opinion review, we interpret these factors not merely as statistical covariates but as pathological surrogates for the virus-host equilibrium. We propose that high baseline hepatitis B surface antigen may reflect a deeply entrenched intrahepatic viral reservoir and may create an antigen-rich environment that impairs effective antiviral immunity. Concurrently, cirrhosis may be better understood as a spectrum of architectural remodeling in which vascular changes and sinusoidal defenestration could limit immune surveillance and affect local therapeutic access. Consequently, treatment durations exceeding 48 weeks may be required, at least in part, to overcome these profound immunological and structural barriers. We advocate shifting future predictive modeling from binary clinical definitions to a granular integration of viral kinetics and histological morphology to refine precision medicine in chronic hepatitis B.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The recent identification of baseline hepatitis B surface antigen and cirrhosis as independent predictors of extended interferon therapy provides a valuable clinical framework, yet the biological mechanisms underlying these associations require further elucidation.
Evidence base and method
In this opinion review, we interpret these factors not merely as statistical covariates but as pathological surrogates for the virus-host equilibrium.
Consensus and conclusions
We propose that high baseline hepatitis B surface antigen may reflect a deeply entrenched intrahepatic viral reservoir and may create an antigen-rich environment that impairs effective antiviral immunity.
Controversies and gaps
Concurrently, cirrhosis may be better understood as a spectrum of architectural remodeling in which vascular changes and sinusoidal defenestration could limit immune surveillance and affect local therapeutic access.
Research and practice implications
Consequently, treatment durations exceeding 48 weeks may be required, at least in part, to overcome these profound immunological and structural barriers.

A 高优先级学术文献 · 综述A high priorityAcademic literature · Review

在经核苷(酸)类似物处理的 慢性乙型肝炎 中添加与改用聚乙二醇干扰素

Add-on versus switch to peginterferon in nucleos(t)ide analogue-treated chronic hepatitis B
作者： Chu Xi
摘要中文翻译该项目包含重现网络荟萃分析的分析数据集和代码:**Chu X、Wang Y、Dai L、Wang L、Liu G、Ji G。在核苷(酸)类似物治疗的 慢性乙型肝炎 中添加与转用聚乙二醇干扰素:权衡病毒学反弹疗效的网络荟萃分析。** 该研究将添加和转用聚乙二醇干扰素策略与持续核(酸)类似物单药治疗的患者进行了比较。慢性乙型肝炎,使用 14 项随机对照试验(网络中的 1,640 名患者)的频率随机效应网络荟萃分析。所有数据均摘自已发表的试验报告;不包括个体患者数据。## 内容 - `nma_data.csv` — 每臂提取的 14 项试验的结果数据 - `nma_analysis.R` — HBsAg 清除率的主网络荟萃分析(联盟表、网络图、森林图、P 分数、节点分裂、净热图、敏感性分析(不包括 He 2016)) - `funnel_v10.R` — 比较调整漏斗图(图 S4)- `review_r1r2.R` — 研究级元回归(治疗 持续时间、基线 HBsAg、NA 持续时间、PEG-IFN 类型、HBeAg 阳性比例)和最后随访敏感性分析(ARES 长期随访;Al Ashgar 2017) - `ga_graphical_abstract.py` — 图形抽象图 (Python/matplotlib) - `README.txt` — 变量定义和运行说明
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注已接受核苷(酸)类似物 治疗 治疗的 慢性乙型肝炎 患者的附加策略与改用聚乙二醇干扰素策略的比较。核心问题是,与持续的核苷类似物单一疗法相比,这些策略在疗效和病毒学反弹方面是否存在差异。
证据基础与方法
该综述对涉及 1,640 名患者的 14 项随机对照试验采用了频率论随机效应网络荟萃分析。所有数据均从已发表的试验报告中提取,不包括个体患者数据。该分析包括排名表、网络图、森林图、P 分数、节点分裂、净热图和敏感性分析(不包括 He 2016)。
共识与结论
该网络荟萃分析比较了 慢性乙型肝炎 患者的附加和转用聚乙二醇干扰素策略与持续核苷(酸)类似物单一疗法。该研究对疗效和病毒学反弹进行了综合评估,结果和结论来自对这些试验的分析。
争议与缺口
该综述承认分析的局限性,包括排除某些研究和潜在的异质性。所使用的方法可能无法完全解决该主题的所有方面,并且在长期随访以及各种因素对 治疗 结果的影响方面存在未解决的问题。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,需要进一步研究添加和转用聚乙二醇干扰素策略的长期影响,特别是与病毒学反弹和 治疗 持续时间相关的影响。此外,需要对基线 HBsAg 水平、NA 持续时间、PEG-IFN 类型和 HBeAg 阳性比例对 治疗 结果的影响进行更详细的研究。

Add-on versus switch to peginterferon in nucleos(t)ide analogue-treated chronic hepatitis B

Authors: Chu Xi
Original abstract (English)
This project contains the analysis dataset and code that reproduce the network meta-analysis reported in: **Chu X, Wang Y, Dai L, Wang L, Liu G, Ji G. Add-on versus switch to peginterferon in nucleos(t)ide analogue-treated chronic hepatitis B: a network meta-analysis weighing efficacy against virologic rebound.** The study compares add-on and switch-to peginterferon strategies against continued nucleos(t)ide analogue monotherapy in patients with chronic hepatitis B, using a frequentist random-effects network meta-analysis of 14 randomised controlled trials (1,640 patients in the network). All data were extracted from published trial reports; no individual patient data are included. ## Contents - `nma_data.csv` — per-arm extracted outcome data for the 14 included trials - `nma_analysis.R` — main network meta-analysis of HBsAg clearance (league table, network plot, forest plots, P-scores, node-splitting, net heat plot, sensitivity analysis excluding He 2016) - `funnel_v10.R` — comparison-adjusted funnel plot (Fig. S4) - `review_r1r2.R` — study-level meta-regressions (treatment duration, baseline HBsAg, NA duration, PEG-IFN type, HBeAg-positive proportion) and last-follow-up sensitivity analyses (ARES long-term follow-up; Al Ashgar 2017) - `ga_graphical_abstract.py` — graphical abstract figure (Python/matplotlib) - `README.txt` — variable definitions and running instructions
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the comparison of add-on versus switch to peginterferon strategies in patients with chronic hepatitis B who are already receiving nucleos(t)ide analogue treatment. The central question is whether these strategies differ in efficacy and virologic rebound when compared to continued nucleos(t)ide analogue monotherapy.
Evidence base and method
The review employs a frequentist random-effects network meta-analysis of 14 randomised controlled trials involving 1,640 patients. All data were extracted from published trial reports, and no individual patient data are included. The analysis includes a league table, network plot, forest plots, P-scores, node-splitting, net heat plot, and sensitivity analysis excluding He 2016.
Consensus and conclusions
The network meta-analysis compares add-on and switch-to peginterferon strategies against continued nucleos(t)ide analogue monotherapy in patients with chronic hepatitis B. The study provides a comprehensive evaluation of efficacy and virologic rebound, with results and conclusions derived from the analysis of these trials.
Controversies and gaps
The review acknowledges the limitations of the analysis, including the exclusion of certain studies and the potential for heterogeneity. The methods used may not fully address all aspects of the topic, and there are unresolved issues regarding the long-term follow-up and the impact of various factors on treatment outcomes.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest the need for further research on the long-term effects of add-on and switch-to peginterferon strategies, particularly in relation to virologic rebound and treatment duration. Additionally, there is a need for more detailed studies on the impact of baseline HBsAg levels, NA duration, PEG-IFN type, and HBeAg-positive proportion on treatment outcomes.

癌症患者的乙型肝炎暴露率下降,但差异仍然存在 - Bioengineer.org

Hepatitis B exposure declines among cancer patients, yet disparities persist - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报巴西最近于 2026 年 10 月 7 日发表的一项研究显示,在 14 年间,癌症患者的 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 暴露量有所下降,携带 HBV 抗体的患者比例从 16.5% 下降至 9.9%。这项研究是在圣保罗州立癌症研究所进行的,分析了 2010 年至 2023 年间接受治疗的近 29,000 名成年癌症患者。尽管整体下降,但差异仍然存在,特别是在男性、老年人和非白人患者中,他们仍然承受着较高的乙肝病毒暴露负担。该研究强调了 HBV 筛查和管理对癌症患者的重要性,因为该病毒是肝癌的重要危险因素,其重新激活可能会破坏 治疗。国际指南建议化疗前进行乙型肝炎筛查,但实施情况仍不一致。研究结果强调了解决与 HBV 相关的健康不平等问题所面临的持续挑战以及改进 疫苗接种 和诊断实践的必要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
研究时间跨度为2010年至2023年,发布日期为2026年10月7日。
地点与对象
该研究是在圣保罗大学医学院附属临床医院的圣保罗州立癌症研究所进行的,重点关注在那里接受治疗的成年癌症患者。
事件
巴西一项为期 14 年的分析发现,癌症患者中 乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV) 暴露量有所下降,但男性、老年人和非白人患者之间仍然存在差异。该研究发表在《癌症原因与控制》杂志上。
规模、影响与风险
在 28,869 名接受测试的患者中,乙肝病毒暴露量从 16.5% 下降至 9.9%。然而,非白人患者和患有血液系统恶性肿瘤的患者没有表现出明显的趋势。HBV 是肝癌的一个强烈危险因素,血液系统恶性肿瘤的再激活率估计为 10%,实体瘤的再激活率为 5%,可能导致急性肝炎、肝功能衰竭和死亡。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究强调了肿瘤中心需要按照国际指南的建议进行一致的乙肝病毒筛查和管理。然而,全球范围内的筛查实施情况仍然不一致,而且调查结果基于单个机构的实验室记录。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B exposure declines among cancer patients, yet disparities persist - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A recent study from Brazil, published on October 7, 2026, reveals a decline in hepatitis B virus (HBV) exposure among cancer patients over a fourteen-year period, with the proportion of patients carrying HBV antibodies dropping from 16.5% to 9.9%. The research, conducted at the São Paulo State Cancer Institute, analyzed nearly 29,000 adult cancer patients treated between 2010 and 2023. Despite this overall decline, disparities remain, particularly among men, older adults, and non-White patients, who continue to bear a higher burden of HBV exposure. The study underscores the importance of HBV screening and management for cancer patients, as the virus is a significant risk factor for liver cancer and its reactivation can disrupt treatment. International guidelines recommend HBV screening before chemotherapy, but implementation remains inconsistent. The findings highlight ongoing challenges in addressing HBV-related health inequities and the need for improved vaccination and diagnostic practices.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, October 7, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Cancer Hepatitis B exposure declines among cancer patients, yet disparities persist by October 7, 2026 in Cancer Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Hepatitis B exposure declines among cancer patients, yet disparities persist Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Hepatitis B virus remains one of the most consequential viral pathogens in oncology, and a new fourteen-year analysis from Brazil shows that while exposure to the virus is steadily falling among people with cancer, it is far from disappearing. Researchers at the University of São Paulo examined nearly 29,000 adult cancer patients treated at a major quaternary oncology center between 2010 and 2023 and found that the proportion carrying antibodies indicating past or present HBV infection dropped from 16.5 percent to 9.9 percent over the study period. The decline, reported in the journal Cancer Causes & Control, is encouraging evidence that vaccination and changing screening practices are reshaping the viral landscape faced by oncologists. Yet the same data reveal stubborn gaps: men, older adults, and non-White patients continue to bear a disproportionate burden of previous HBV exposure, and a substantial share of admitted cancer patients were never tested at all. The clinical stakes of this epidemiology are considerable. Hepatitis B virus infects hepatocytes and can establish a chronic infection that progresses to cirrhosis and hepatocellular carcinoma; infection is recognized as one of the strongest risk factors for liver cancer, raising the odds of that malignancy by roughly 47-fold. Globally, an estimated 240 million people live with chronic HBV infection. For cancer patients, the virus carries an additional threat: systemic anticancer therapies suppress immune function, which can permit viral replication to resume even in patients whose infection appeared resolved. This reactivation is driven by covalently closed circular DNA, a persistent viral reservoir that remains within the nucleus of hepatocytes long after serological recovery. A recent global meta-analysis estimated reactivation rates of approximately 10 percent among patients with hematological malignancies and 5 percent among those with solid tumors. Reactivation is not a benign event. It can force interruption, delay, or modification of cancer treatment precisely when therapy is most needed, and it may culminate in acute hepatitis, liver failure, and death. For this reason, international guidelines recommend screening for HBV before patients begin chemotherapy or other immunosuppressive regimens. Patients who test positive for the hepatitis B surface antigen should receive antiviral therapy with agents such as entecavir, tenofovir disoproxil fumarate, or tenofovir alafenamide, while those who are surface-antigen negative but positive for antibody against the core antigen, known as anti-HBc, should be considered at risk of reactivation and managed with antiviral prophylaxis or serological and molecular monitoring. Despite these recommendations, screening remains inconsistently implemented across oncology centers worldwide. The São Paulo study drew on laboratory records from the São Paulo State Cancer Institute, affiliated with the Clinical Hospital of the university’s Faculty of Medicine, which has treated more than 140,000 cancer patients since opening in 2008. Of 125,985 patients admitted between 2010 and 2023, 28,869, or 22.9 percent, underwent anti-HBc testing. The mean age of those tested was 56 years, roughly two-thirds were women, and 61.4 percent self-identified as White. Solid tumors accounted for 71.2 percent of the cohort, with breast cancer the most common diagnosis, while hematological malignancies made up 22.2 percent, led by non-Hodgkin lymphoma and multiple myeloma. HBV exposure was defined as anti-HBc positivity, a marker that indicates prior contact with the virus but does not by itself distinguish resolved infection from active disease. The headline finding is a statistically significant downward trend in seroprevalence, from 16.5 percent in 2010 to 9.9 percent in 2023. The decline was broad but uneven. Among men, seroprevalence fell from 25.1 percent to 12.3 percent, a steeper drop than among women, whose rates fell from 11.4 percent to 8.3 percent. Every age group improved, including patients aged 18 to 39, whose seroprevalence fell from 7.7 percent to 2.4 percent, and those aged 60 and older, whose rates declined from 20.1 percent to 13.7 percent. Seroprevalence also fell significantly among White patients and among those with solid tumors. By contrast, no significant trend emerged among non-White individuals, whose rates moved only from 21.4 percent to 17.7 percent, or among patients with hematological malignancies, whose rates remained essentially flat at around 13 percent. Multivariable logistic regression sharpened the demographic picture. Patients aged 60 or older had 4.4-fold higher odds of HBV exposure than those under 40, men had 89 percent higher odds than women, and non-White patients had 25 percent higher odds than White patients. The age gradient likely reflects cumulative lifetime exposure in cohorts born before universal neonatal vaccination, compounded by the reduced immunogenicity of hepatitis B vaccines in older adults. The sex difference may have biological underpinnings: androgens exert immunosuppressive effects, while estrogens appear to enhance immune and vaccine responses, potentially leaving men less able to clear or control the virus. The ethnic disparity points toward inequities in social determinants of health, including unequal access to vaccination, diagnosis, and treatment, a pattern consistent with prior Brazilian and international studies. Cancer type itself was not independently associated with exposure, but subgroup patterns were striking. Among hematological malignancies, non-Hodgkin lymphoma showed a seroprevalence of 15.5 percent, a malignancy repeatedly linked to HBV in meta-analyses, though the mechanisms remain unresolved. Among solid tumors, the highest rates appeared in sarcoma, at 43.5 percent, a figure the authors caution may be inflated by small numbers, and in liver and bile duct cancers, at 31.2 percent. In adjusted analyses, liver and bile duct cancers carried more than threefold higher odds of HBV exposure, and gastrointestinal cancers carried 41 percent higher odds, findings consistent with the established oncogenic role of the virus, which operates through chronic hepatic inflammation, integration of viral DNA into the host genome, and the activity of viral proteins such as HBx. Within the anti-HBc-positive group, additional testing of subsets clarified the virological picture. The hepatitis B surface antigen was positive in 7.8 percent of those tested, indicating current infection, while 73.2 percent carried anti-HBs antibodies consistent with resolved infection. Among 484 patients who underwent HBV DNA testing, 25.6 percent had detectable viral load. Taken together, most anti-HBc-positive patients showed evidence of past exposure, but a meaningful minority carried molecular or serological signs of active infection, underscoring why confirmatory testing matters before immunosuppressive therapy begins. One encouraging operational trend accompanied the epidemiological one: the proportion of admitted patients screened for anti-HBc rose from 11.1 percent in 2010 to 29.2 percent in 2023. Even so, more than two-thirds of cancer patients admitted over the period were never tested, and global data suggest screening before chemotherapy reached only about 57 percent in pooled estimates, with uptake particularly weak among patients with solid tumors. The authors note that their retrospective, single-center design has limits, including incomplete medical records, unknown testing indications, and possible selection bias, and that treatment-related immunosuppression may have blunted antibody detectability in some patients. Still, the scale of the cohort and the length of the observation window lend weight to the conclusion. As vaccination coverage expands and screening becomes more systematic, the residual burden of HBV exposure among cancer patients should continue to shrink, provided that prevention reaches the demographic groups the data show are still being left behind. View five news elements
Time
The study period spans from 2010 to 2023, with the publication date on October 7, 2026.
Location and population
The research was conducted at the São Paulo State Cancer Institute, affiliated with the Clinical Hospital of the University of São Paulo’s Faculty of Medicine, focusing on adult cancer patients treated there.
Event
A fourteen-year analysis from Brazil found a decline in hepatitis B virus (HBV) exposure among cancer patients, though disparities persist among men, older adults, and non-White patients. The study was reported in the journal Cancer Causes & Control.
Scale, impact and risk
HBV exposure decreased from 16.5% to 9.9% among 28,869 patients tested. However, non-White patients and those with hematological malignancies showed no significant trend. HBV is a strong risk factor for liver cancer, with reactivation rates estimated at 10% for hematological malignancies and 5% for solid tumors, potentially leading to acute hepatitis, liver failure, and death.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study highlights the need for consistent HBV screening and management in oncology centers, as recommended by international guidelines. However, screening remains inconsistently implemented globally, and the findings are based on laboratory records from a single institution.

表观遗传编辑可以消灭 慢性乙型肝炎 感染 - SingularityHub

Epigenetic Editing Could Wipe Out Chronic Hepatitis B Infections - SingularityHub
新闻简报SingularityHub 于 2026 年 10 月发表的一项开创性研究强调了一种新的表观遗传编辑方法,该方法有可能消除 慢性乙型肝炎 感染。该方法由 nChroma Bio 及其合作者开发,通过添加化学标签来关闭基因,而不改变病毒 DNA 本身,从而靶向感染小鼠和猴子中的 HBV DNA。三个月剂量后,大约 90% 的小鼠在至少六个月内没有表现出病毒迹象,并且对猴子的安全测试没有发现明显的副作用。治疗 称为 CRMA-1001,使用 CRISPR-Cas9 系统的修改版本来传递 DNA 甲基化的遗传指令,主要针对肝脏。虽然该团队并未称其为治愈方法,但结果表明它可以替代 慢性乙型肝炎 每日服用的 抗病毒药物 药丸。该方法还具有治疗其他顽固病毒的潜力。第一位人类患者正在正在进行的 1/2 期试验中接受治疗,需要进一步的研究来确认其对人类的有效性。该方法针对两种形式的 HBV DNA(共价闭合环状DNA 和整合 DNA),已知它们可以逃避 治疗 并导致肝损伤和癌症风险。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 10 月报道,其中包括在小鼠和猴子中进行的 1/2 期临床试验的结果,并提到了今年早些时候正在进行的试验中第一位接受治疗的人类患者(N1、N18)。
地点与对象
该研究以小鼠和猴子作为实验模型,治疗 正在人体中进行测试,特别是那些患有 慢性乙型肝炎(N12、N14、N15、N18)的人。
事件
科学家们开发了一种表观遗传沉默方法来靶向感染小鼠和猴子中的 HBV DNA,该方法有可能取代 慢性乙型肝炎(N12、N14、N15、N17)的每日 抗病毒药物 药丸。
规模、影响与风险
治疗 在小鼠身上显示出有希望的结果,六个月后 90% 的小鼠维持了不可检测的病毒载量。它还证明了对猴子的安全性,没有明显的副作用。潜在影响包括帮助数百万人接受 慢性乙型肝炎 治疗,并解决与 HBV(N14、N15、N23、N24、N27、N28)相关的肝癌和肝硬化风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
该团队并没有称其为治愈方法,治疗 仍处于正在进行的 1/2 期临床试验中。该方法被认为是一个潜在的突破,但需要在人体上进行进一步测试(N16,N18)。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Epigenetic Editing Could Wipe Out Chronic Hepatitis B Infections - SingularityHub

News Brief
A groundbreaking study published in October 2026 by SingularityHub highlights a new epigenetic editing approach that could potentially eliminate chronic hepatitis B infections. The method, developed by nChroma Bio and collaborators, targets HBV DNA in infected mice and monkeys by adding chemical tags that switch the genes off without altering the viral DNA itself. After three monthly doses, approximately 90% of the mice showed no signs of the virus for at least six months, and safety tests in monkeys found no notable side effects. The treatment, called CRMA-1001, uses a modified version of the CRISPR-Cas9 system to deliver genetic instructions for DNA methylation, primarily targeting the liver. While the team did not call it a cure, the results suggest it could replace daily antiviral pills for chronic hepatitis B. The approach also has potential for treating other stubborn viruses. The first human patient was treated in an ongoing Phase 1/2 trial, and further research is needed to confirm its effectiveness in people. The method addresses both forms of HBV DNA—cccDNA and integrated DNA—which are known to evade treatment and contribute to liver damage and cancer risk.
Original source text (English)
Biotechnology Epigenetic Editing Could Wipe Out Chronic Hepatitis B Infections An experimental therapy kept the virus at bay for at least six months. The approach could target other stubborn viruses too. Shelly Fan Oct 06, 2026 Image Credit Colorized models of hepatitis B viruses / NIAID on Unsplash Share Viruses are sneaky things. When they die, their DNA often sticks around. Some of them transform into freely roaming DNA that evades treatment. Others tunnel into their host’s genome and lie dormant, sometime for decades. But dormant doesn’t mean gone. When the time is right, they strike. If a patient with hepatitis B virus (HBV) or HIV stops taking antiviral drugs, it can trigger a severe rebound of the disease. Stress, aging , and other factors that dampen the immune system can also reactivate the chickenpox virus, triggering the painful, blistering rash known as shingles. Now, scientists have found a way to shut down one of these biological sleeper agents. Called epigenetic silencing , the technology hunts down lingering HBV genes in infected mice and adds a chemical tag that switches the genes off, without cutting the viral DNA. Unlike gene editing tools that shred genetic material, potentially causing collateral damage to the host’s genome, the epigenetic approach could be safer but still long-lasting. After three monthly doses, roughly 90 percent of the mice showed no signs of the virus for at least six months after their final shot. A safety test in monkeys found no notable side effects, including any signs of a runaway immune response. The team stopped short of calling it a cure. But if proven to be as helpful in humans, the injections could replace daily pills charged with keeping the virus in check and help millions of people laden with chronic hepatitis B. Earlier this year, the first patient was treated with the formulation in an ongoing Phase 1/2 clinical trial , which is testing for safety and early efficacy. If successful, the work could lead to treatments that silence the DNA of other viruses too. “This differentiated mechanism has the potential to enable durable antiviral control,” wrote the team at nChroma Bio, a biotechnology company based in Boston, and collaborators. Shape Shifter Chronic HBV infection is a silent killer. A person can feel totally healthy. Meanwhile, the virus repeatedly cycles between dormant and active states, triggering the immune system to attack infected cells in the liver. Over time , the repeated assaults leave scar tissue that slowly replaces the organ’s healthy architecture and causes it to malfunction. Even with its considerable regenerative capability, the liver struggles to do its usual work, from making enzymes that break down food to producing proteins that help blood clot. Blood pressure in the digestive system skyrockets, increasing the risk of deadly stomach bleeding. Even more insidious, the virus increases the risk of liver cancer, sometimes before notable tissue damage or symptoms. When HBV DNA integrates into the host’s genome, it can alter nearby gene activity, disrupt tumor-suppressing genes, or even rearrange chromosomes . Meanwhile, constant inflammation can further fuel the development of cancer. There are ways to fight back: Vaccines prevent infection, and antivirals suppress the virus. These drugs mimic the DNA letters HBV needs to replicate. Once incorporated into the viral genome, they block any further copying. But they’re a Band-Aid, not a cure. Stop taking them, and HBV rebounds in nearly everyone thanks to its two stubborn forms of DNA. One of these is called cccDNA, a tiny, chromosome-like structure that sits inside infected cells and acts as a reservoir for the virus. It’s “the driver of HBV persistence,” wrote the team. The other is viral DNA that has integrated into the host genome. These fragments continuously churn out viral proteins, which train the host’s immune system to recognize them as friendly. Whole viruses can then escape surveillance and continue replicating. “Thus, effective therapies for chronic hepatitis B must ultimately address both forms of viral DNA,” the team wrote. Easy Silence Previous methods have used various gene editors to hunt down and destroy viral DNA. In mice and monkeys, these tools have suppressed viral loads by up to 96 percent , with some effects lasting over a year. Be Part of the Future Sign up to receive top stories about groundbreaking technologies and visionary thinkers from SingularityHub. 100% Free. No Spam. Unsubscribe any time. The approach nChroma is developing, CRMA-1001, works differently. Rather than cutting viral DNA with the gene editor CRISPR-Cas9 or rewriting DNA with base editing, nChroma turned to epigenetics, the system of chemical tags that control whether genes are turned on or off. The new treatment mimics an epigenetic process for silencing genes called methylation. It combines genetic instructions to build a DNA methylation enzyme with a version of Cas9, stripped of its cutting ability. Here, Cas9 acts like a molecular docking system, while a guide RNA directs the machinery to a specific site in the HBV genome. All components are packaged into lipid nanoparticles. After injection into the bloodstream, they predominantly travel to the liver and methylate the viral DNA reservoir and integrated HBV DNA. In mice, a single dose slashed viral DNA activity and HBV surface protein levels more than a thousandfold. Among animals given three monthly doses, roughly 90 percent maintained undetectable viral load after six months. Adding standard antivirals to the mix lowered viral DNA levels even further. The team then tested escalating doses in cynomolgus monkeys for safety. At the highest dose, liver enzymes rose in the blood, suggesting the liver’s cells were stressed or injured. But the levels returned to normal within four weeks of the last dose. The animals didn’t show clinical symptoms of liver damage, such as anatomical changes or blood-clotting issues. The therapy has now entered the clinic . The team launched a trial in Hong Kong and received authorization to begin trials in New Zealand and the United Kingdom. They have competition too. In May, Seattle-based Tune Therapeutics reported early results from an ongoing phase 1/2 trial of its own epigenetic silencer. After a single dose, several HBV-associated biomarkers plummeted for at least 17 months, and none of the participants experienced severe side effects. In Shanghai, China, Epigenic Therapeutics is pursuing a similar strategy. Its formulation showed promise in preclinical models and is advancing toward a clinical trial , although details on how their silencer works are scant. How long the effects will last is an open question. Can patients eventually stop taking antivirals and be functionally cured? And whether the therapies can silence the full range of HBV variants, whose genomes can differ by more than eight percent , is also unknown. Then there’s the risk these therapies go rogue and methylate other parts of the host genome with unintended consequences. Costs could pose another challenge. Most people with chronic hepatitis B live in less economically developed regions in Southeast Asia and Sub-Saharan Africa, where access to expensive, specialized treatments may be limited. Still, “epigenetic editing is an exciting, reversible solution that can long-term silence viral repertoires without the genotoxicity of gene editing,” wrote Marleen van der Laan at the University of Groningen and colleagues in an independent review. “Its success in functionally curing chronic hepatitis B is highly promising, but further steps are required to ensure its applicability.” Shelly Fan Shelly Fan Dr. Shelly Xuelai Fan is a neuroscientist-turned-science-writer. She's fascinated with research about the brain, AI, longevity, biotech, and especially their intersection. As a digital nomad, she enjoys exploring new cultures, local foods, and the great outdoors. Hepatitis B vaccine study condemned as unethical wins approval from Danish university - CIDRAP

News Brief
A Danish university has approved a CDC-funded study in African newborns, where only half of the babies will receive the hepatitis B vaccine. The study has been condemned as unethical, raising concerns about its methodology and ethical implications. However, the report does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers related to the hepatitis B virus. The approval of the study highlights ongoing debates about vaccine ethics and public health interventions, but the lack of detailed health impact data means the risk and scale of the virus's effects are not quantified. The source is a reputable media outlet, and the confidence level is moderate due to the absence of explicit health impact metrics.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

健康研究所警告称,需要更多检测来减少乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎感染 - NL Times

More testing needed to reduce hepatitis B and C infections, warns Health Institute - NL Times
新闻简报荷兰国家公共卫生与环境研究所 RIVM 警告称,需要进行更多检测来减少乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎感染。该研究所提出了一项计划,增加对东欧、中南美洲、非洲和亚洲等肝炎较常见国家的人群进行检测。这种方法类似于结核病检测政策,来自高 患病率 地区的个体会定期接受检测。RIVM 还强调更好地保存记录以识别和解决医疗保健系统中的弱点的重要性。尽管取得了进展,但报告指出,由于缺乏资金和 Covid-19 大流行的影响,十年前设定的所有目标并未实现。每年有 450 至 500 人死于乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎,而数千人多年来一直不知道自己已感染病毒。慢性感染可导致肝硬化和肝癌等严重疾病。该计划是更新后的国家肝炎计划的一部分,RIVM 呼吁改进有关乙型肝炎 疫苗接种 的公共信息,该信息已经适用于幼儿,但可以扩大到成人。
查看新闻五要素
时间
荷兰必须加强对抗乙型和丙型肝炎的斗争,国家公共卫生与环境研究所(RIVM)的专家表示。该报告于周一发布,但发布日期为2026-10-06。
地点与对象
荷兰,重点关注寻求庇护者、男男性行为者、吸毒者和被拘留者。荷兰的幼儿接种了乙型肝炎疫苗。
事件
RIVM 警告称,需要进行更多检测来减少乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎感染。该研究所制定了一项进一步减少病毒感染的计划,呼吁对来自肝炎相对较常见国家的人进行更多检测。
规模、影响与风险
每年,有 450 至 500 人死于乙型肝炎和丙型肝炎。成千上万的人在不知情的情况下携带该病毒多年,因为症状出现较晚。存在肝硬化的风险,进而增加患肝癌的风险。
应对、状态与不确定性
RIVM 需要一个记录诊断和 治疗 的中央系统。该计划是2016年发布的国家肝炎计划更新的一部分。疫苗接种的公共信息需要完善。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

More testing needed to reduce hepatitis B and C infections, warns Health Institute - NL Times

News Brief
The RIVM, the Netherlands' National Institute for Public Health and the Environment, has warned that more testing is needed to reduce hepatitis B and C infections. The institute has proposed a plan to increase testing among people from countries where hepatitis is more common, such as Eastern Europe, Central and South America, Africa, and Asia. This approach is similar to the tuberculosis testing policy, where individuals from high-prevalence regions are routinely tested. The RIVM also emphasizes the importance of better record-keeping to identify and address weaknesses in the healthcare system. Despite progress, the report notes that not all goals set a decade ago have been met due to a lack of funding and the impact of the Covid-19 pandemic. Each year, 450 to 500 people die from hepatitis B and C, and thousands remain unaware of their infection for years. Chronic infections can lead to serious conditions like cirrhosis and liver cancer. The plan is part of an updated National Hepatitis Plan, and the RIVM is calling for improved public information about hepatitis B vaccination, which is already available for young children but could be expanded for adults.
Original source text (English)
Skip to main content Main navigation Top stories Health Crime Politics Business Tech Culture Sports Weird 1-1-2 Top stories Health Crime Politics Business Tech Culture Sports Weird 1-1-2 Image A doctor is comforting her patient - Credit: royalty / DepositPhotos - License: DepositPhotos Health Dutch National Institute for Public Health and the Environment National Institute for Public Health and the Environment National Institute of Public Health and the Environment Public Health and the Environment RIVM National Institute for Public Health and the Environment hepatitis B Hepatitis C » More tags « Less tags Share this article: Opens in a new window Opens in a new window Opens in a new window Opens in a new window Opens in a new window Opens in a new window More testing needed to reduce hepatitis B and C infections, warns Health Institute Tuesday, 6 October 2026 - 21:10 The Netherlands must step up its fight against hepatitis B and C, experts at the National Institute for Public Health and the Environment (RIVM) say. The health institute has drawn up a plan to further reduce the viral infections, calling for more testing of people from countries where hepatitis is relatively more common. That covers a large part of the world, from countries in Eastern Europe to Central and South America, Africa, and Asia. One option the experts cite is testing asylum seekers for hepatitis after they arrive in the Netherlands. This is similar to the policy regarding tuberculosis, as anyone from a country where the disease is widespread is routinely tested. Better record-keeping could also yield gains. The RIVM wants a central system in which diagnoses and treatments are recorded. “With this insight, weak spots in care can be identified and addressed more quickly,” the explanation says. The plan is part of an update to the National Hepatitis Plan released in 2016. Despite progress, not all of the goals set a decade ago have been met, in part because of “a lack of funding and the Covid-19 pandemic,” according to the report released Monday. Each year, 450 to 500 people die from the effects of hepatitis B and C. Thousands of people carry the virus for years without knowing it, because symptoms appear only later. In addition to migrants, men who have sex with men, people who use drugs, and people in detention face a higher risk. Young children in the Netherlands are vaccinated against hepatitis B. Vaccination is also possible later in life. Public information about that needs to improve, the experts say. There is no vaccine against hepatitis C. The disease can usually be treated effectively, but the infection must be detected first. Hepatitis is an inflammation of the liver that occurs in several forms. Infections often cause no symptoms. With hepatitis B and C, however, chronic infections can develop and lead to serious illness. There is a risk of cirrhosis of the liver, which in turn raises the chances of liver cancer. Other symptoms include fatigue, fever, muscle pain, loss of appetite, and jaundice, in which the skin and eyelids, among other body parts, turn yellow. Location and population
The Netherlands, with a focus on asylum seekers, men who have sex with men, people who use drugs, and people in detention. Young children in the Netherlands are vaccinated against hepatitis B.
Event

Scale, impact and risk
Each year, 450 to 500 people die from the effects of hepatitis B and C. Thousands of people carry the virus for years without knowing it, because symptoms appear only later. There is a risk of cirrhosis of the liver, which in turn raises the chances of liver cancer.
Response, status and uncertainty
The RIVM wants a central system in which diagnoses and treatments are recorded. The plan is part of an update to the National Hepatitis Plan released in 2016. Public information about vaccination needs to improve.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

AusperBio to Present Long-Term Follow-Up Data of AHB-137 Treatment in Chronic Hepatitis B in Two Late-Breaking Oral Presentations at AASLD The Liver Meeting® 2026 - PR Newswire

News Brief
AusperBio Therapeutics, Inc. is set to present long-term follow-up data on AHB-137 treatment for chronic hepatitis B at AASLD The Liver Meeting® 2026, which is scheduled for October 7, 2026. The presentation is part of a broader effort to evaluate the drug's efficacy, following its completion in global Phase 1 and multiple Phase 2 studies. AHB-137 is currently undergoing a Phase 3 trial in China, indicating ongoing research into its potential as a treatment for hepatitis B. The event is not an outbreak or public health incident but rather a scientific update. No specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers are reported in the evidence. The information is based on clinical trial results and is being shared at a medical conference, highlighting the drug's progress in development.
Original source text (English)

Inquiries Editorial Bureaus Partnerships Media Inquiries Worldwide Offices Hamburger menu Send a Release ALL CONTACT INFO Contact Us 888-776-0942 from 8 AM - 10 PM ET AusperBio to Present Long-Term Follow-Up Data of AHB-137 Treatment in Chronic Hepatitis B in Two Late-Breaking Oral Presentations at AASLD The Liver Meeting® 2026 News provided by AusperBio Therapeutics Inc. Oct 07, 2026, 20:30 ET Share this article Share toX Share this article Share toX Two abstracts on long-term follow-up data of AHB-137 treatment for chronic hepatitis B have been selected for late-breaking oral presentations at The Liver Meeting® 2026. SAN FRANCISCO, Oct. 7, 2026 /PRNewswire/ -- AusperBio Therapeutics, Inc. and Ausper Biopharma Co., Ltd. (together, "AusperBio"), a near-commercial biopharmaceutical company, today announced that two abstracts reporting long-term follow-up data of AHB-137 treatment have been selected for late-breaking oral presentations at The Liver Meeting® 2026, the annual meeting of the American Association for the Study of Liver Diseases (AASLD), to be held November 5–9 in Denver, Colorado, USA. The presentations will report long-term follow-up from the AB-10-8004 study (NCT07146100) in two populations of participants with chronic hepatitis B (CHB) who completed prior AHB-137 clinical studies: nucleos(t)ide analogue (NA) treatment-experienced patients, and treatment-naïve patients who received AHB-137 monotherapy. "We are honored that two abstracts from the AB-10-8004 study have been selected for late-breaking oral presentations at The Liver Meeting®," said Chris Yang, Co-founder and CSO of AusperBio. "We look forward to sharing these long-term follow-up data with the hepatology community as we continue to advance AHB-137 toward our goal of a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B." The presentation details are as follows: Publication Number: 5001 Abstract Title: Durability of AHB-137 up to 9 Month Post End-of-Studies in Nucleos(t)ide Analogue Treated Participants with Chronic Hepatitis B: First Long-Term Follow-up Report from the AB-10-8004 Study Session: Late Breaking Abstract Parallel Session Date and Time: November 8, 2026, 2:00 pm – 2:15 pm MT Presenter: Jinlin Hou, MD, Southern Medical University, Guangdong, China Publication Number: 5010 Abstract Title: Durability of Functional Cure to AHB-137 Monotherapy in Treatment-Naïve Participants with Chronic Hepatitis B: Long-Term Follow-Up Results of the AB-10-8004 Study Session: Late Breaking Abstract Parallel Session 2 Date and Time: November 8, 2026, 6:00 pm – 6:15 pm MT Presenter: Junqi Niu, MD, The First Hospital of Jilin University, Changchun, China Late-breaking abstracts will be available on the AASLD website beginning at 8:00 am MT on November 5, 2026. AusperBio plans to issue a press release with additional details following the presentations. The full scientific program for The Liver Meeting® 2026, as well as the abstracts, can be found on the AASLD website. About AHB-137 AHB-137 is AusperBio's lead investigational unconjugated antisense oligonucleotide (ASO) therapy for chronic hepatitis B (CHB), developed using the Company's proprietary Med-OligoTM ASO platform. AHB-137 is designed to suppress hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) production, inhibit viral DNA replication, and promote immune reactivation, with the goal of achieving a functional cure for CHB. AHB-137 has demonstrated promising clinical results and is currently being evaluated in a Phase 3 trial in China, following completion of a global Phase 1 study and multiple Phase 2 studies. The Company is advancing AHB-137 through a coordinated global development strategy toward potential commercialization. AHB-137 is an investigational product candidate and has not been approved by any regulatory authority. About AusperBio AusperBio is a near-commercial biopharmaceutical company focused on developing differentiated oligonucleotide therapeutics and targeted delivery technologies, with an initial focus on achieving a functional cure for chronic hepatitis B. The Company's innovation engine is built on its proprietary Med-OligoTM ASO and Au-HALOTM targeted delivery platforms, supporting the development of therapies across multiple oligonucleotide modalities. With multiple clinical-stage programs and a growing pipeline, AusperBio is advancing a backbone-based combination strategy for functional cure of CHB while exploring opportunities in additional disease areas with significant unmet medical needs. For more information, visit www.ausperbio.com. Forward-Looking Statements This press release has been prepared by AusperBio (the "Company", "We") for informational purposes only. 世卫组织确认亚美尼亚已达到区域乙型肝炎控制目标 - Armenpress

WHO validates Armenia as having reached regional hepatitis B control targets - Armenpress
新闻简报经欧洲技术咨询专家组 (ETAGE) 确认,世界卫生组织 (WHO) 已确认亚美尼亚已通过其免疫计划达到了区域乙型肝炎控制目标。这一成就反映了亚美尼亚在过去二十年中一贯政策和措施的成功,包括在新生儿和儿童中普及乙型肝炎疫苗接种。验证过程涉及研究疫苗接种覆盖指标和乙型肝炎患病率,目的是减轻该疾病给个人和国家带来的负担。世界卫生组织欧洲区域办事处强调了严格的评估过程,而亚美尼亚卫生部长Anahit Avanesyan则指出,该证书是一个重要的里程碑,证明了国家免疫规划的有效性。她强调需要继续疫苗接种努力并设定新目标。亚美尼亚国家疾病控制和预防中心 (NCDC) 也对一致的国家和医疗保健政策、医疗保健专业人员的奉献以及国际合作伙伴的支持表示认可。这一成就被描述为免疫和乙型肝炎预防方面的重要一步,并计划在未来将该计划扩大到包括孕妇。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该活动于 2026 年 10 月 6 日进行了报道,这一成就是通过过去二十年严格的评估过程得到确认的。
地点与对象
亚美尼亚,重点关注国家免疫规划以及儿童和青少年人口。
事件
世界卫生组织 (WHO) 确认亚美尼亚通过免疫工作已达到区域乙型肝炎控制目标,欧洲技术咨询专家组 (ETAGE) 也证实了这一点。
规模、影响与风险
亚美尼亚实现了乙型肝炎的高 疫苗接种 覆盖率,预防了围产期 传播 并减少了疫苗接种群体中 HBsAg 的 患病率。这一成就被描述为免疫和乙型肝炎预防工作的重要一步,对于减轻该疾病给个人和国家带来的负担具有重要意义。
应对、状态与不确定性
验证过程确认了这一成就,并向亚美尼亚颁发了正式证书。世卫组织欧洲区域办事处强调了严格的评估过程,亚美尼亚卫生部计划继续开展 疫苗接种 工作,并将该计划扩大到包括孕妇。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

WHO validates Armenia as having reached regional hepatitis B control targets - Armenpress

News Brief
The World Health Organization (WHO) has validated Armenia as having reached regional hepatitis B control targets through its immunization program, as confirmed by the European Technical Advisory Group of Experts (ETAGE). This achievement reflects the success of Armenia's consistent policies and measures over the past two decades, including universal hepatitis B vaccination among newborns and children. The validation process involved studying vaccination coverage indicators and the prevalence of hepatitis B, with the goal of reducing the disease's burden on individuals and the state. The WHO Regional Office for Europe highlighted the rigorous assessment process, while the Armenian Minister of Health, Anahit Avanesyan, noted that the certificate is a major milestone, testifying to the effectiveness of the national immunization program. She emphasized the need to continue vaccination efforts and set new targets. The National Center for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC) of Armenia also acknowledged the journey of consistent state and healthcare policies, the dedication of healthcare professionals, and the support of international partners. The achievement is described as an important step in immunization and hepatitis B prevention, with plans to expand the program to include pregnant women in the future.
Original source text (English)
Yerevan 9 °C USD / AMD 361.97 English ArmeniaEconomyWorldCultureSportsFact CheckExclusive Projects ... PhotosVideos newsfeed Armenia12:50, 6 October 2026 WHO’s ETAGE validates Armenia as having reached regional hepatitis B control targets 8 minute read Armenia has been validated by the European Technical Advisory Group of Experts (ETAGE) as a country that has reached the regional targets for hepatitis B control through immunization. ETAGE is a body of 6–8 immunization experts appointed by the WHO Regional Director for Europe. This group is tasked with providing independent review and expert technical input to WHO/Europe’s vaccine-preventable diseases and immunization programme (VPI), with the goal of facilitating and accelerating achievements in relation to European Immunization Agenda 2030, including eradication, elimination and control of vaccine-preventable diseases in the WHO European Region. In connection with this achievement, an official certificate was presented to Armenia at the initiative of the World Health Organization (WHO) Country Office in Armenia. Within the framework of the assessment conducted by the World Health Organization, key factors included high vaccination coverage against hepatitis B, prevention of perinatal transmission, and ensuring a low prevalence of HBsAg among vaccinated groups. The achievement is an important step in Armenia’s immunization and hepatitis B prevention efforts and reflects the results of the consistent policies and measures implemented by the Government of the Republic of Armenia and the Ministry of Health. Minister of Health of the Republic of Armenia Anahit Avanesyan, welcoming those present at the ceremony, said that the certificate is a major achievement and testifies that the national immunization program is working and is already producing results. “Our children are free from acute hepatitis B and this very severe chronic disease. This is first and foremost a health issue, but it is also an economic issue. Every month, a person living with chronic hepatitis B spends 30,000-40,000 drams just on medication. Imagine what a huge burden this is for each person and family, as well as for the state. By investing in the health of our children, we are keeping them free from this disease and the costs associated with it in the future. I think this is a very significant result, and I congratulate and thank the entire healthcare system, our healthcare workers, healthcare organizers and the Ministry for this achievement,” the minister said. Avanesyan said that achieving good results does not mean stopping; it is necessary to move forward and set new targets. “Of course, this is an occasion to look back and see that our policies and efforts are producing results, and to move forward with the aim of embracing even greater targets. We are also now discussing the issue of providing medicines for the management of chronic hepatitis B, because it is very important that our patients are also protected in terms of their ongoing treatment. First of all, we plan to consider including pregnant women in this program, and later expanding it as well. But we must continue the very important work of vaccination and the overall immunization program. These achievements can be very easily lost; we must preserve them and improve them even further,” the minister said. Dr. Roberta Pastore, Lead for Immunization Programme Strengthening at the WHO Regional Office for Europe, emphasized that she was happy to be in Armenia and to represent the WHO Regional Office for Europe, noting that Armenia has achieved the hepatitis B control targets through vaccination. According to her, in 2015, within the framework of the European Vaccine Action Plan, the WHO Regional Office for Europe declared hepatitis B control a priority. It continues to remain a goal within the European Immunization Agenda 2030, as well as within regional action plans for the elimination of viral hepatitis. “The achievement of the hepatitis B control targets is confirmed through a rigorous assessment process, within which vaccination coverage indicators and the prevalence of hepatitis B are studied. Armenia has undertaken all the necessary steps to collect evidence confirming the achievement of the hepatitis B control targets. The validation process in Armenia confirmed that over the past two decades, the country’s efforts have been successful in introducing universal hepatitis B vaccination among newborns and children and ensuring high vaccination coverage,” Pastore said. According to her, this proves that Armenia has not only reached its hepatitis B control target, but has gone beyond it, moving closer to the goal of eliminating the disease. “I would like to congratulate you on this achievement, which makes Armenia one of 11 validated countries. Thanks to your commitment to and investment in vaccination over the past two decades, you have been able to create a generation of children and adolescents who will grow up to be healthier adults. They will be free from hepatitis B infection and its severe and fatal consequences,” she said. Dr. Stepan Atoyan, Director General of the National Center for Disease Control and Prevention (NCDC) of the Ministry of Health of the Republic of Armenia, speaking about the path to achieving the target, noted that this is not only a summary of the work done over the past years, but also a journey based on consistent state and healthcare policies, the dedicated work of healthcare professionals and the support of international partners. “We know that hepatitis B vaccination was introduced into the national immunization schedule about 30 years ago and, like any newly introduced vaccine, went through its subsequent stages: first rejection, then partial acceptance, and finally acceptance. As a result, we now have the picture that was mentioned by our colleague. Social studies that have been conducted also attest to this success. Acute hepatitis B is no longer being recorded among children, which speaks to the success of the vaccination process. At the same time, I would like to note that this certificate is, of course, very binding, because, as Ms. Avanesyan mentioned in her remarks, last year we recorded a fairly high rate among children under one year of age, and we are obliged to maintain this high rate and, naturally, preserve it,” Atoyan said. He also thanked the minister for her personal participation in the digitalization process, as the digitalization of healthcare services greatly contributes to the immunization process, as well as to epidemiological surveillance and disease control. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

被谴责为不道德的乙型肝炎 疫苗 研究获得丹麦大学批准

Hepatitis B vaccine study condemned as unethical wins approval from Danish university
新闻简报由美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 资助、在几内亚比绍进行的一项有争议的乙型肝炎 疫苗 研究,尽管存在伦理问题,但已获得南丹麦大学的批准。该研究计划仅向 14,000 名登记新生儿中的一半提供 疫苗,但其设计招致了国际批评。该批准是在修订后的方案获得丹麦大学和几内亚比绍道德委员会的道德许可后获得的。然而,在获得几内亚比绍政府的最终授权之前,研究无法开始。该国乙型肝炎感染率很高,近五分之一的感染者,15%至25%的慢性感染者死于肝癌或衰竭。该研究因伦理问题而被暂时搁置,非洲疾控中心尚未提供详细评论。倡导团体谴责这项研究侵犯人权,并正在探讨采取行动阻止其实施。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于 2026 年 10 月获得南丹麦大学批准,早前的担忧已于 2026 年 12 月提出。该研究将在几内亚比绍获得必要的国家授权后恢复,但该授权尚未获得批准。
地点与对象
这项研究是在几内亚比绍进行的,该国乙型肝炎感染率很高(接近五分之一的人)。目标人群是新生儿,登记婴儿人数为 14,000 名。
事件
由 CDC 资助、在几内亚比绍进行的一项有争议的乙型肝炎 疫苗 研究,尽管存在伦理问题,仍获得了南丹麦大学的批准。该研究计划仅向一半的登记婴儿提供 疫苗,这引起了国际社会的批评。
规模、影响与风险
几内亚比绍的乙型肝炎感染率很高,15%至25%的慢性感染者死于肝癌或衰竭。该研究的设计被谴责为不道德,导致该研究暂时搁置。
应对、状态与不确定性
由于伦理问题,该研究被搁置了几个月。南丹麦大学已批准该研究,但尚未获得几内亚比绍政府的最终授权。非洲疾病预防控制中心了解这项研究,但尚未提供详细评论。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Hepatitis B vaccine study condemned as unethical wins approval from Danish university

News Brief
A controversial hepatitis B vaccine study in Guinea-Bissau, funded by the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), has been approved by the University of Southern Denmark despite ethical concerns. The study, which plans to offer the vaccine to only half of the 14,000 enrolled newborns, has drawn international criticism for its design. The approval came after a revised protocol received ethical clearance from both the Danish university and Guinea-Bissau's ethics committee. However, the study cannot begin until final governmental authorization is obtained in Guinea-Bissau. The country has a high hepatitis B infection rate, with nearly one in five people infected, and 15% to 25% of those with chronic infections dying from liver cancer or failure. The study's ethical issues have led to a temporary hold, and the Africa CDC has not provided detailed comments. Advocacy groups have condemned the study as a violation of human rights and are exploring actions to prevent its implementation.
Original source text (English)

Topics & Projects Antimicrobial Stewardship Chronic Wasting Disease Osterholm Update Podcasts Newsletters About CIDRAP CIDRAP in the News Our Staff Our Director Osterholm in the Press Shop Merchandise Contact Us About Search CLOSE Search Support Menu Hepatitis B vaccine study condemned as unethical wins approval from Danish university Liz Szabo, MA Hepatitis Childhood Vaccines Tiago_Fernandez/iStock Share Copied to clipboard The University of Southern Denmark confirmed that it has given the green light to a controversial study in Guinea-Bissau, funded with $1.6 million from the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC), that plans to offer life-saving hepatitis B vaccines to only half of enrolled babies. News of the study, first reported in December by CIDRAP News, prompted an international outcry from health groups and political leaders, who condemned its design as unethical. Those concerns led Guinea-Bissau officials and administrators at the Danish university to place the study on hold for several months. Nearly one in five people in Guinea-Bissau are infected with hepatitis B, which kills 15% to 25% of people with chronic infections by causing cancer or liver failure. Vaccinating babies at birth is safe and can prevent lifelong infections. Yesterday, citing unnamed sources, Rolling Stone broke the news that the Danish university had approved the clinical trial and that CDC leaders were pushing for the study to resume. The Department of Health and Human Services, which oversees the CDC, did not respond to questions before press time. The Danish researchers leading the study in Guinea-Bissau, Peter Aaby, PhD, and Christine Stabell Benn, MD, PhD, could not be reached for comment. In response to earlier criticism, their research group, the Bandim Health Project, defended their choice to withhold the birth dose of hepatitis B vaccine from half of the 14,000 newborns to be enrolled in the clinical trial. In a statement on Bandim’s website, researchers wrote, “No child enrolled in the study will receive fewer vaccines than under current national policy in Guinea-Bissau,” a developing country with far fewer resources than the United States and Europe. Guinea-Bissau does not currently vaccinate newborns against hepatitis B, as recommended by the World Health Organization, but plans to begin in 2028. University official cites revised study protocol An official at the University of Southern Denmark confirmed to CIDRAP News that he granted approval yesterday to proceed with the study, in spite of widespread ethical concerns. Professor Jakob Grauslund, PhD, MD, dean of the university’s faculty of health sciences, said he approved the study based on “an independent ethical assessment” carried out by the school’s research ethics committee. “The revised protocol also received ethical approval from the relevant ethics committee in Guinea-Bissau this summer,” Grausland said. “Final governmental authorization in Guinea-Bissau has, however, not yet been granted, and the study cannot begin until the necessary national authorization has been obtained.” An official from the Africa Centres for Disease Control and Prevention (Africa CDC) had little to say when asked about the study at a Thursday morning press conference. “There was ongoing discussions between the US government, who is supporting this research, and also the government” of Guinea-Bissau, said Wessam Mankoula, MD, a medical epidemiologist and acting director of Africa CDC’s Center of Public Health Emergency. “We can give you later updates about the most recent development as to the implementation of the study.” Stand Up for Science, a nonprofit advocacy group, vowed to stop the Danish study. “This is a gross violation of human rights and the use of bad science against some of the most vulnerable people in the world,” said Colette Delawalla, MD, the group’s founder and CEO, in a press release. Location and population

Event
A controversial hepatitis B vaccine study in Guinea-Bissau, funded by the CDC, was approved by the University of Southern Denmark despite ethical concerns. The study plans to offer the vaccine to only half of the enrolled babies, which has drawn international criticism.
Scale, impact and risk
Hepatitis B infection rates in Guinea-Bissau are high, with 15% to 25% of people with chronic infections dying from liver cancer or failure. The study's design has been condemned as unethical, leading to a temporary hold on the research.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was placed on hold for several months due to ethical concerns. The University of Southern Denmark has approved the study, but final governmental authorization in Guinea-Bissau has not been obtained. The Africa CDC is aware of the study but has not provided detailed comments.
原文 / Source
Published: 2026-10-05  |  Publisher: Rijksinstituut voor Volksgezondheid en Milieu | RIVM

National Hepatitis Plan: More effort is needed to prevent and reduce hepatitis B and C - Rijksinstituut voor Volksgezondheid en Milieu | RIVM

News Brief
The RIVM published a report on the National Hepatitis Plan on October 5, 2026, highlighting the need for increased efforts to prevent and reduce hepatitis B and C infections. The report is an official statement and does not describe a specific outbreak or event. It focuses on policy considerations rather than providing detailed data on cases, deaths, or exposures. The report does not mention any specific risk levels or response measures, such as testing or contact tracing. The content is general and does not include any confirmed or suspected incidents related to hepatitis B or C. The RIVM is the source of the report, and it is part of their broader public health initiatives.
